Yeah. It’s like… I can sleep the whole night, or at least be in bed the whole night, and still feel completely wiped out.

I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think so? Not in a clear way like that.

Sometimes I get this thought that people at work would probably have an easier time if I wasn’t slowing things down, but then I start thinking about how last week the printer jammed three times and everyone blamed the toner even though it was the paper tray, and I was the one who fixed it, so… I guess that’s not really what you asked.

Death feels like a big word. I don’t sit there planning anything, if that’s what you mean. I just have moments where I kind of wish everything would stop for a while. Does that count?

Work stuff gets in my head sometimes, but then I’ll be thinking about one receipt I maybe filed wrong and suddenly I’m remembering this teacher in high school who used to make us redo the whole assignment if the margins were off—which is not really related, sorry.\n\nI guess it kind of all blends together lately, but I don’t know if it’s different things exactly or just the same few things looping around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms lately, like restlessness, feeling on edge, muscle tension, or being easily fatigued?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly tired, I guess. Like, even if I technically spend the whole night in bed, getting from the parking lot into the office feels ridiculous, like I’m dragging sandbags or something. I don’t know, even walking in feels like too much sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends, no longer feel satisfying or make you feel happy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t really paid attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what your mood feels like most days—like, is there a constant heaviness or sadness, or does it come and go depending on what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Most days lately it’s more like I wake up and it’s already there, this heavy, empty feeling. Like before anything even happens, before I check my phone or make coffee or whatever.\n\nAnd it’s weird because I’ll be sitting in my condo, which I worked really hard to get, and I should feel proud or settled or something… but instead I just feel like I could cry. Like the walls are fine, the place is fine, I picked the little kitchen shelves myself—sorry, that’s not really important. It just feels heavy even when nothing specific is going wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything you do anymore that truly makes you feel good or brings you a sense of pleasure, even for a little while?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to go to salsa class, and that was fun even when I was terrible at it—there was this one instructor who always counted too loud, which somehow helped—but I stopped going.\n\nAnd I still have my paints sitting out on the table at home. They’ve been there long enough that I probably should clean them up, but I just look at them and feel… nothing. Like they belong to somebody else, not me. So I guess things that used to make me feel good don’t really do much anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving much slower than usual or, the opposite, feeling so restless that you can't sit still most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think both, kind of. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even press the buttons. Which is stupid because it’s literally my job, you know, numbers and receipts and all that.\n\nBut then other times I’m the opposite. I’ll be fidgeting with the receipts, lining them up or messing with the edges, and I can’t really sit still. We have these little paper clips too, and I’ll keep bending them without realizing it—sorry, that’s probably not the point. So yeah, slower sometimes, restless other times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you don’t have any energy left, even if you haven’t done anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s like… I can sleep the whole night, or at least be in bed the whole night, and still feel completely wiped out.\n\nEven just walking from the parking lot into the office feels ridiculous, like my legs are dragging sandbags or something. And it’s not even a long walk—there’s this one spot by the little tree where people always park crooked, and I get annoyed about that, but anyway… by the time I’m at my desk I already feel tired.\n\nBy lunch, my brain feels worn out too. Like I haven’t done anything that should exhaust me, but I feel used up.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying, or if it would be better if you weren’t around anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think so? Not in a clear way like that.\n\nSometimes I get this thought that people at work would probably have an easier time if I wasn’t slowing things down, but then I start thinking about how last week the printer jammed three times and everyone blamed the toner even though it was the paper tray, and I was the one who fixed it, so… I guess that’s not really what you asked.\n\nDeath feels like a big word. I’m not really sure. I don’t wake up thinking, like, “I wish I hadn’t woken up,” exactly.

It’s more like… the morning feels heavy sometimes, and I’ll lie there thinking about everything I have to do, like getting dressed and getting to work and whether I remembered to put the receipts in the right folder—because if I don’t, it turns into this whole mess later.
